ParlaMint-AT_1997-04-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00071_d7e2265	I ask for the reply, Federal Chancellor.
ParlaMint-AT_1997-04-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00071_d7e2281	Ladies and gentlemen! Leave I should like to point out, first of all, that it is less important for the citizens of Austria in connection with this federalism discussion, how bureaucracy is pushed back and forth or something like that. We want to address this state reform in accordance with two essential criteria. The first: How can we improve the quality of public service for citizens? – By providing services as close to citizens as possible. And the second: How can we improve efficiency? – By combining decision-making responsibility with financial responsibility, by removing multiple competences, overlaps and the like. In this sense, I fully declare myself a bourgeois federalism. But it is also – and I believe this is an essential step – to see that, in the sense of this consultation mechanism, we have already created a new quality of financial cooperation between the Federal Government and countries. In the sense of this constructive cooperation, we have now decided, on the basis of the Perchtoldsdorf Agreement – and this is undisputed – to make the effort to rethink the federal and state governments together, to explore the administration at their various levels, and to look at where we can achieve a reduction in costs and better efficiency by clarifying competence, by clear unbundling, by reducing the proximity of citizens, by reducing confusion, by reducing confusion. The Federal Government has already nominated a working team for this, and it will immediately take up work with the working team of the countries in this sense.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-04-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00071_d7e3417	Federal Chancellor! My question is: [[/PAKT/VHG/XX/M/M_00120/index.shtml -- 120/M]] Are you advocating a ban on the use of genetic engineering in Austrian food production by the corresponding amendment of the Austrian Food Act?
ParlaMint-AT_1997-04-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00071_d7e3445	Federal Chancellor, please.
ParlaMint-AT_1997-04-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00071_d7e3460	Mrs. Dr. Petrovic! I now find it very easy to welcome the quality of the consent, following the results of the genetic engineering people's desire, since I have already said before that the direction of this popular desire is also personally supported by me. However, we should come to the heart of the matter: Today, we have not allowed international agreements that prohibit the import of food containing substances adapted in its gene chain. At international level, however, we will be very committed to improving the rules and the framework. It cannot be allowed to say simply clearly and openly that we cannot prohibit imports of foodstuffs mixed in their production with substances modified in sub-areas. That is why we have known ourselves to offer Austrians a clear and comprehensive label, so that they have the opportunity to clearly recognize genetically modified foods and to decide for themselves what they are taking from the shelves. Everything else would be false, because it would give the impression that genetically modified foods could be kept away from Austria. I am against the deception of the population, and therefore we are in favour of a comprehensive labelling obligation.
ParlaMint-AT_1997-04-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00071_d7e3486	Would you like an additional question? - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_1997-04-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00071_d7e3502	Federal Chancellor! The Novel Food Regulation gives the impression that labels are not effective. The Austrian labelling regulation also does not work in practice – I have not seen a single label yet. But a ban under Austrian food law is absolutely possible. Do you not see that it would be a competitive advantage for Austrian agriculture and for Austrian food production if all goods were "Made in Austria" free of genetic engineering?
ParlaMint-AT_1997-04-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00071_d7e3517	Mr. Federal Chancellor.
ParlaMint-AT_1997-04-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00071_d7e3532	Mrs. Dr. Petrovic! I have done this because I can well imagine that Austrian agriculture can achieve a positive marketing effect by saying: Our production is free of genetically modified substances, precursors and more. In the sense of a positive marketing concept, for example in the field of organic farming, it should be considered that it is clear that those products which are offered on the market by Austrians – from seed to feed to finished product – do not prove to contain genetically modified substances. [[Abg. Rossmann: That means "manipulated", not "modified"!]] That would be a concrete, positive marketing mark in your sense.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-04-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00071_d7e5255	Mr President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! The Federal Constitutional Act on Cooperation and Solidarity in the Sending of Units and Individuals to Foreign Countries or that, as it will be called in the short notice, KSE-BVG is an important law far beyond what is now related to it and which may be the first use, namely Albania. It has rightly been pointed out that a large number of preparations have taken place and that, in particular, it has been a matter of adapting the existing law on postings to the changed situation, such as participation in the "Partnership for Peace", Common Foreign Policy et cetera, and also the formulation of "international organisations". Finally, this will also enable the sending body to send not only closed quotas from members of the Federal Army, from members of the guards of the Federal Government and from persons who have committed themselves to the service for the use in question, but also to be able to send individuals abroad. For example, such civil experts can be used in the event of disasters, but also election observers and others may fall under certain conditions. It also takes into account the extended comprehensive security concept of the new Enforcement Act, and it also takes into account the intention of Austria and many other countries and also organizations not only to act when something has already happened, but also to prevent the handling of crises or causes that can lead to wars from giving aid, to monitoring, to sending people who observe something that mediate and thereby contribute to the fact that there is no need for military use at all. It is one of the most important aspects of international work to use all this in instruments and possibilities to promote all that helps prevent wars, and not simply to focus on intervening. There was a broad consensus on the draft, and it is actually regrettable that there was a consensus on the basic intentions, on many changes – indeed, there were changes to the government template, because in some respects it was vague – that about so many things – about the intention and about many details – and that there was no agreement on individual points that only play a subordinate role in comparison to the whole. It is sad that this must then lead to the rejection of the whole law, of all the thoughts, of the draft as such from the opposition. I would now like to comment on the various points. One of the main points of the public debate was what Mrs Pollet-Kammerlander said yesterday or yesterday in an issue that this Entrepreneurship Act 'was 'disposing' of neutrality', and in a newspaper interview she added a little more. Yes, yes, Mrs. Colleague, what do you not do "daily everything" to get into the paper! It is not true, however, that through this Entrepreneurship Act, neutrality is touched or put out of power or, as you call it, to draw a particularly plastic image, is "disposed". The formulation "in accordance with the obligations of international law" is also not such a synonym that is used so that one does not have to use the word "neutrality", as well as in the famous history of Böll "Dr. Murkes collected silence", in which he replaces the expression "God" by "the higher being we worship" and always uses this long, not so clear synonym. So it is not true that instead of 'neutrality', we say 'national obligations'. I would have had nothing against mentioning neutrality, but to say that it was "disposed" or only replaced by this unspeakable formulation, so that one would not have to use the word "neutrality" as if it were toxic – that does not correspond to the facts. Already in the explanations, it was clearly pointed out that the term "national obligations" is more than just a synonym of neutrality, which includes several things to be considered. At least – but not exclusively – this is Article 2 Z5 of the United Nations Statute; these are further decisions of the Security Council in accordance with Chapters VI and VII of the United Nations Statute; these are common positions and joint actions of the European Union under Articles J.2 and J.3 of the Treaty for the European Union; these are the generally recognised rules of international law in accordance with Article 9 para. 1 Federal Constitutional Act, for example, is also the requirement for the international consent of the host State regarding the granting of the right of residence; this is also the principle of international cooperation, such as in principle IX in the Final Act of Helsinki, the Conference on Security and Cooperation in Europe, in the Declaration on the principles governing the relations of the participating countries; and of course these are also the content of the ever-increasing neutrality of international law standards. Nor is it true, Mrs Kammerlander, that what you are saying and what has been said in newspapers is that only the OSCE and the other things are included in the list, but not even in the description of what is under the term 'national obligations', which would refer to neutrality. I recommend that you turn to page 8 of the government template, take your finger and count first, second, third, fourth, fifth, sixth, seventh, eighth, ninth, tenth, fourth, in the eleventh line. [[the speaker demonstrates this]] [[Abg. Wabl: This is eleven! You cheated! It is clear that consideration should also be given to neutrality. We have also stated in a committee that this law does not impose any further obligation to contribute. [[Calls to the Greens.]] You can scream as much as you want, you can claim what you want. [[Abg. We have also made clear in two committee statements other misinterpretations, especially those which have already been said in advance, there are still these or those mistakes. In any case, a very broad approach can be taken. This broad spectrum includes measures of preventive diplomacy, peacekeeping and peacekeeping measures, as well as peace-building measures in the period of conflict. This whole package of measures is terminological, conceptual, United Nations under the term "Security of Peace in the Spirit of the Charter of the United Nations". We wanted to clarify this, and I believe that this clarification would have been necessary, so that we do not restrict ourselves to the narrow concept of peacekeeping by military measures after a ceasefire, but that we should make an active contribution to peacekeeping already in the run-up to preventive, diplomatic measures. However, we also wanted to be stabilising in the period of conflict through concrete measures. This has been missed, and I believe that this has not served us well. ParlaMint-AT_1997-04-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00071_d7e6245	Mr President. High house! The new Entrepreneurship and Solidarity Act is one of the fundamental legislative answers to a fundamental change in the strategic situation in Europe. There is a transition from a situation with high risk and high stability to a situation with lower risk, but also much lower stability. The new law should help us to make a solidarity contribution, so that we can also make a contribution from the Austrian point of view to the most important security policy objective of this continent and thus also Austria. The objectives at the strategic level have changed in the form that the deterrence and defence of an armed war are no longer in the foreground, but that it is in particular a question of preventing the outbreak of wars and war-like crises on the continent as early as possible, in order not to allow for a spread at all and thus to initiate and secure a phase of peace and stability for our continent. There is no doubt that the sending of international troops has a particularly high priority here. Not least, we all had to experience this urgently in the Yugoslavian conflict. We have had to register that the community of states has reacted far too late and that this late reaction has created a situation in which hundreds of thousands of people have been killed or raped, hundreds of thousands of millions of people have been driven out and immense cultural property destroyed in the long term. At the same time, this also created a situation which will undoubtedly help to put the constant crisis centre of the Balkans at enormous risk in its stability. [[Abg. May. Stadler: There the NATO has created order and not your comical troops!]] As a direct border to the Balkans, Austria has an immense vital interest in the stability of this region in the immediate vicinity of our homeland. ParlaMint-AT_1997-04-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00071_d7e7036	Mr President. Federal Minister! Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! We know that since 1960 more than 36 000 Austrian soldiers, executives and civilians have participated in 25 United Nations peacekeeping missions. Today, Austrian quotas in Cyprus, at the Golan Heights and in Bosnia are fulfilling their tasks for peace. Not to be forgotten are the many observers who, in numerous missions around the world, are helping to maintain peace. It is precisely preparing a unit for use in Albania. – Mr Federal Minister, I hope that this is not easy to use very carefully. All of this, ladies and gentlemen, proves to me that we Austrians are not away, but that we are in solidarity with international peacekeeping within the framework of our possibilities. It is therefore incomprehensible to me that a particular side is repeatedly spoken of as "safety-political riding". This is not only unfair, but also detrimental to the reputation of our country in the world. It's just not right! The opposite is the case. It is in line with a long-standing Austrian tradition of contributing to the maintenance of peace and security. The change in security policy that has taken place in Europe has not been able to remain without any effect on the applicable legal regulations for the deployment of deployments. We know that the law, which was created in 1965, provides only an insufficient legal basis for participation in peacekeeping, disaster aid and humanitarian measures. This new legal regime will be used to remedy this situation. In particular, it will also be possible to participate in the preparatory measures for training abroad. Mr Schieder has already pointed out voluntaryity in this context, so I would like to stress that voluntary participation in peacekeeping operations is a matter for all members of the Federal Government, i.e. soldiers, both military and professional soldiers. I think we are well advised to stick to it. There can therefore be no general obligation for foreign operations as a condition for a service relationship as a professional soldier. The new legal situation also allows Austrian soldiers to be sent abroad for exercises and training – this is also our agreement. I should also like to say that this is in itself nothing new – these projects have so far been carried out on a mission basis. In the future, however, it will also be assumed that the training of the units, which are mostly made up of conscripts, is in principle carried out in Austria. Exercises of units abroad can therefore only be exceptional cases and will always need a very special reason. As regards the exercises in the "Partnership for Peace": It should only take part in those groups of persons who have declared their voluntary participation in such missions and who also have a classification among the preparatory units. Put to the point: It is crucial to participate in PRP exercises that they are not elements of the forces of the present forces, but are units preparing foreign operations. [[Congratulation of the Abg. Scheibner.]] Federal Minister! National defence must be adapted to the new requirements of European security. It is therefore necessary to answer the questions: What will the military instrument require in the future, what importance does classical defence have, and what requirements now come from the international peacekeeping area? – If there is to be a list of priorities, then there will be top priority for maintaining regional stability, but also for international solidarity, followed by assistance in the national framework, and only then will there be the likelihood of military intervention in border security and defence. New ways must be taken to deal with these new developments. This concerns not only the size and organisational structure of the army, but also the underlying doctrine. Federal Minister! A modern legal basis has been created for international solidarity. Now you are on the train to ensure the availability of preparatory units of the federal army by means of appropriate organizational measures. There is still a lot to be done in this area, and we will be happy to support it. We believe that these preparatory units should be recruited in a military structure consisting of volunteers of the present forces, the militia and the reserve, as well as from civilians. In this context, I would also like to say quite clearly and clearly: Internationally applicable associations for combat operations are not to be set up by the Austrian Federal Army! Although the army must be able to provide faster, more predictable, more reliable than previously specialists and troops for solidarity efforts, the construction of constantly prepared troops is not necessary because of the international timetables – we all know – for this. In addition to these organisational measures, it will also be necessary to ensure the required number of volunteers through an incentive system. But even there we do not have to invent the wheel; I must point out the Swedish model, which is very successful practice. Similarly, we could design it in Austria. In conclusion: Federal Minister! With the increase of international requirements, new and responsible tasks come to the Federal Army. We Socialists are willing to support all developments and projects in order to find new answers with the help of flexible, contemporary military structures to the questions of European security, because participation in actions of international solidarity is not only a worthwhile task for the benefit of people, but also a very useful task for the benefit and reputation of our country. And that is because we will give this law our approval.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-04-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00071_d7e8424	Dobar then, poštovane lady i gospodo! Dobar then, gospodin President! [[Abg. Dr. Pumberger: What does that mean?]] Dobar then, Dr. Khol! Dr. Karlsson: Hvala ljepa!]] [[Hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, hey, ……]] Ladies and gentlemen! I would like to take advantage of this opportunity for the first reading of an initiative by the Greens in order not to miss out on the most important current national political issues. First of all, a brief explanation of our short, but very profound, and well-founded application. What we are striving for is to be seen, above all, in the light of the national and minority-political discussions in recent years, mainly by the tragic events for the Austrian minorities, by letter bomb terrorism and by the murder attack in Oberwart, which called for four deaths. After these tragic events, there has been a great deal of sympathy in Austria, but also a far greater interest in minority policy issues than ever before. In recent years, I myself have received so many questions relating to my role as a Green minority spokesperson as never before. This is very, very pleasing for me, because the interest in groups of people in Austria has increased, and because now, probably – I suppose – it has also spoken all the way to the west of Austria, that the Burgenland is a region in which people who speak four different languages, so four native languages, are now aware of the fact that there are the Roma and Sinti peoples in Austria who have already been recognized as a people's group before these terrible events. But what is explicitly not available is a commitment by the Republic to linguistic diversity in that country. Therefore, in all parties and, above all, in the national councils of the six recognised groups, a discussion has been held about this – the Greens were not entirely indifferent – how this commitment by the Republic to the promotion, preservation and respect of this linguistic and cultural diversity could also be anchored at the legal level in Austria. This proposal for an initiative, which provides for an amendment to Article 8 of the Austrian Constitution and gives a clear signal. Why exactly Article 8? – That’s easy to explain. Article 8 therefore, because Article 8 regulates the national language of the Republic, and the national language of the Republic is German, as is the language of the National Council. Other languages can only be used here if you quote. [[Abg. Ing. Tychtl: Except for the welcome!]] Except for the greeting, this has become, so to speak, a right of habit. I am also very grateful to the President for this. Everyone else can do that too. This amendment to Article 8, proposed by us, aims to ensure that the position in which the groups and minorities in Austria have been and are still in the position of the bitters, in the position of those who always have to go to court, in order to enforce rights which must always fight for rights, in order to obtain them, is thus facilitated by the Republic of Austria making this confession and setting it in the Constitution. That is the idea behind this request. We didn't think of something completely new: Switzerland has already made exemplary progress in this regard last year. The population of Switzerland has expressed itself last year in a referendum with overwhelming majority voting in favour of a similar state target, as it contains this request – a state target for minority protection. At the time, the government and the parliament in the cantons have so justified this vote template, which is the basis of the referendum, that I do not want to abstain from you, so that you do not feel that something is happening now, which is a generous act of the Republic towards minorities and groups of people: "Our state has always understood itself as a peaceful and solidaric union of four language communities. Switzerland is rightly proud of its ability to preserve its linguistic and cultural diversity. It is one of the tasks of the Federal Government to preserve Switzerland's four-lingualism and to promote mutual understanding among the language communities. Federal and cantons are expressly obliged to promote understanding and exchange between the four language communities." – This is only a small part of the reason for the vote. In the light of this Swiss model, it is the corresponding demand of all ethnic groups – it was only last week that there was the first joint meeting of all the advisory committees, and that this was unanimously approved – that a state target of this kind should be included in the Austrian Constitution. And to place it in such a prominent place of the federal constitution, of course, has a special meaning for the Austrian minorities, because it represents something like an appreciation of our existence. – That is the content of this request. But with this request – should he receive the approval of the National Council – only the beginning of these minority policies and thus also the legislative acts in the ethnic group sector is set. The implementation of European minority law standards, the ratification of the two conventions, namely the European Charter on the Protection of Regional and Minority Languages and the Framework Convention on the Protection of National Minorities, is urgently needed. It sets out very relevant national rights in the area of school, media, topography and cultural promotion. We have already unanimously invited the Federal Government in January 1996 to ratify these two international documents. It has not yet happened, perhaps today is a reason for the whole thing to get a boost. The third area – and this is actually the most difficult because it is the most concrete for the ethnic groups – is the re-codification of the People's Group Act in line with – and I would like to add that explicitly – the wishes and needs of the groups. And there is a great task that we have to deal with, because the year 1976, from which the People's Group Act originates, is simply overcome by groups of people, minorities, even at European level. And today, the law from 1976 actually presents itself to us, when one sees it very critically, as – as I have already referred to it – the intertwined relationship of the Republic with its peoples cast into sections. At that time, the political environment, the discussions and the local panel tower in Carinthia led to this law, today – 21 years later – an adaptation is urgently necessary. This is – and I would just like to list this recently – primarily about the unanimous desire of the groups for legal equality of all recognised groups at the level of Article 7 of the Vienna State Treaty. At present, as you know, only Croatians and Slovenians are in it and enjoy this protection. Then it goes on to call for the creation or promotion of a bilingual education system in the multilingual regions of Austria – that is, where groups live – from kindergarten to matura. There is also a very important requirement for the training of the educational staff, because there cannot be a bilingual teaching without bilingual teachers; bilingual kindergartens without bilingual kindergartens, of course, cannot. The third point is the reorganisation of the national councils. There has already been an initiative by Dr. Khol and Dr. Kostelka here in 1995. To this day, it has not been further developed at the law level. This includes things – and I have said this several times – that I and the Greens are absolutely in favour of. However, only one point has been included in this initiative; the other parts that are essential for the revision of the People's Group Act are missing. The fourth point is the creation of bilingual or multilingual media, both in audiovisual and in print, in some areas. This is about the support of private media on the one hand and, of course, – and this is very important, and the minorities will always stick to it – also to the extension of the public-law offer. It is essential for me, in the course of the discussion on privatisation in the radio and television sectors, to note that private operators cannot be taken out of duty here. The fact that the ORF has to make bilingual television programmes within the framework of the public service contract – which is not yet established – is, as it were, a voluntary service of the ORF – to oblige the ORF to send bilingual minority and thus non-quota programme, but perhaps not to give private radio and, in the future, TV operators this edition is an absolute distortion of competition. I regret that the Regional Radio Act has failed to include a provision which also requires private radio operators to support linguistic diversity in the regions in which they operate radio by offering foreign-language – Slovenian, Croatian, Hungarian – programmes. The fifth point is the implementation of bilingualism in the field of administration and topography – keyword: 25 percent clause. We have resisted massively when there have been percentage clauses for the Hungarian minority in Slovakia. And you know that the Slovak system is beyond all European minority protection. At the time, we Austrians have made massive protests, but in their own country we have 25 percent clauses, which are absolutely no longer timely. The last point is one that is not yet affected at the legal level, namely consistent structural improvement and economic promotion measures, with particular reference to independent regional development in bilingual areas. The minorities in Austria do not live in the middle of Austria, now spoken in the transferred sense, but always in the peripheral and thus poorest areas, in southern Carinthia, in southern Burgenland and in southern Styria. It is the unanimous view of all groups that it is necessary, in the field of economic promotion and structural measures, to have specific possibilities to take them into account. One last point – this one has nothing to do with a legislative innovation and with a state objective – which I would like to address concerns the miserable causa of the Styrian Slovenians. I am now in the National Council for seven years, and since my entry into the National Council, it has always been suggested that the Styrian Slovenians should be greatly appreciated and recognized as a minority and a people's group. [[Abg. Dipl.-Ing. Schöggl: How many are there?]] However, the Styrian state government and, as the controller, the Styrian head of state are still opposed to the Styrian Slovenians, who are expressly mentioned in Article 7 of the Vienna State Treaty, the Magna Charter of Minority Protection in Austria, to grant the appropriate opportunities. This, I think, will be quite slowly a grotesque, especially since the demand for representation of this group, for example, in the People's Advisory Council, is still not implemented by sitting and voting. This is something we call good-will, because there is no need for legal action, not even in the way of the regulation. Therefore, ladies and gentlemen, I would like to call on all of you, especially the colleagues from the ÖVP, to ask and ask for pressure on the Styrian government in their respective party, so that this sad issue is once off the table and there is a public recognition act as soon as possible. I am very optimistic about the fact that we will be able to adopt both a state target and a novel to the People's Group Act in Parliament this year. That would really make the move into the next national group policy millennium!

I said it in my last speech here and I say it again: It is high time we were given these two conventions for ratification. In conclusion, I believe that there is a changed, encouraging and positive climate in relation to the issues of the ethnic groups. We should use this momentum for positive changes. This also includes a state target, which we consider to be positive and for which we are in principle expressing our views.

ParlaMint-AT_1997-04-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00071_d7e14540	The Federal Minister for Finance has also made a statement. - Please, Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_1997-04-17-020-XX-NRSITZ-00071_d7e14561	Mr President. High house! Ladies and gentlemen! I would just like to make a few comments on the clarification. Although the title of the Agreements is 'Investment Protection Agreements', I would rather, in my semantic sense, believe that this does not mean, of course, that an investment made is absolutely protected by the Republic of Austria. Under the title "Investment Protection Agreements", we try – as many other countries do – to create favourable conditions for foreign investment through bilateral agreements and, as far as possible, to secure foreign investment with the respective contractual partner, as far as possible. I would like to say very clearly: If, apart from Mr Schreiner, there is someone else who believes that an investment protection agreement is a guarantee of liability from the Republic of Austria, I would ask him not to understand that. However, I would like to express my opinion that this agreement is indeed about important materials, and I am therefore very glad that we will be given a very broad support for it. These agreements are aimed at establishing economic relations with countries which are very moving from their internal political substance, namely, favourable framework conditions and international safeguards. This includes the agreement of the most favourable and equal treatment with domestics, which must not be neglected, the guarantee of free transfer to freely convertable currencies, a comprehensive protection of expropriation, including something very important, a duty of compensation and the guarantee of justice by means of an agreement of international arbitration. However, I believe – and I should like to say this with all clarity – that, of course, the economy also means taking risks. I don't think that's against anyone. The economy is competitive, the markets are open worldwide, and as a state, we can only try to create framework conditions within which economic action by private Austrian companies in China, Ukraine or anywhere in order will be taken into account. I would like to add that, of course, and I have also pointed out in committee, the agreement with Hong Kong has been concluded in agreement with the People's Republic of China, with which we also have such an agreement. This means, of course, that this agreement continues to apply after the political changes in Hong Kong in the middle of 1997. I hope that the necessary clarifications have been made, and I would like to ask for your consent.
